Its pieces are `Banner/Label`, `Title`, `LevelRow` / `CoinsRow` / `MaterialRow1` / `MaterialRow2` (each with a `*Shadow` copy; `Icon`, `Label`, `Check`, `Have`; a gate's `materials` fill the material rows, set 2026-10-19 to 20 Oak Log + 20 Stone for World 2, and rows past its list hide), and `Buy` (`Content`: `Label`, `Coin`, `Amount`; `PressScale`) with `BuyLip`. State colours are `FillTop_` / `FillBottom_` / `Lip_` / `Ink_<Met|Locked|Unlocked>` and `ShortColor` attributes on the gui. The `SignBoard` part is invisible, so only the rounded sign shows. The template's text is realistic placeholder ("World 2", "Buy" 5,000), and its UIPaddings hold the FontDrop correction, so the sign is centred in Edit mode too. Measured: the banner within 1 px, Buy within 2.
  - **Buying:** `Client.UI.GateUI` fills the sign from Level / Coins / HighestWorld and fires `Remotes.GateBuy { id }` when both requirements are met. `GateService` re-checks everything (within `BUY_RANGE` 40 of the board), spends the coins, calls `PlayerDataService.unlockWorld` and saves. For a player whose HighestWorld reaches the world, every gate part with the **`OpensWithGate`** attribute turns invisible and walk-through, locally: the curtain, and since 2026-10-11 the `SignBoard` and `Rope_L/R` too, and the sign gui switches off (user: the sign goes once bought). **Hover (2026-10-11):** while Buy can be pressed, hovering grows it and `BuyLip` (its `HoverScale`) by the gui's `HoverGrow` (1.05) and lightens the fill by `HoverLighten` (0.18); Buy and BuyLip are centre-anchored. Playtested: 6,000 → 1,000 coins, HighestWorld 2, curtain open, sign "Unlocked!".
- **Unlocking a world plays a short cinematic (issue 28, 2026-10-25, the user's picks: a flight over the world, the boss's name card slam, the unlock burst at the gate, letterbox bars with the world title, a click to skip).** `Client.Controllers.WorldFlybyController` plays it when the local player's `HighestWorld` rises after `DataLoaded` (buying a gate; an admin unlock too), for a world with a flight path: (1) **unlock** (`UnlockSeconds` 1.4): `DeathPop` stars, a `Poof` and a gold ring at the gate as GateUI opens it, the HUD hidden (every PlayerGui ScreenGui but `Cinematic`, `WorldBanner`, `ScreenFade`, put back as it was), the player frozen (`InputLocked`, anchored, controls off; a `Cinematic` attribute hides the guide arrow), letterbox bars sliding in and "WORLD 2 / UNLOCKED!" slamming in over turning rays while the camera eases up and back; (2) **flight**: the camera glides at one speed with a smootherstep ease at both ends (no speed or acceleration jump) along a Catmull-Rom path from where it was through the world's hand-placed `Workspace.Worlds.<world>/Cinematic/Flyby/P1, P2...` (invisible parts: move them to change the route). The `Flyby` folder's attributes: `Mode` "Straight" (one heading the whole way, P1 to the last P) or "Path" (it turns with the path, looking `LOOK_AHEAD` 40 ahead), `Pitch` (degrees it looks down), `Seconds` (the flight's length; else the gui's `FlightSeconds` 7), `BossTurn` (the last share of the flight over which the view turns onto the boss: World 3's 0.15, so its last stretch ends looking at the Spider Queen instead of past her into the hall's corner). The view direction goes through two cascaded exponential filters (`LOOK_SMOOTH` 0.35 s), so turns build up and settle softly. **Smoothed (the same day, the user: World 2's first flight was "too movey", "too much shaking"; "really smooth", it may be longer):** measured at real speed, World 2 now turns at most 11.7 degrees a second and peaks at 115 studs a second with no frame-to-frame jumps. A camera more than `FAR_START` (120) from P1 (the admin replay pressed anywhere) starts just behind P1 behind a quick fade instead of flying across the map. The world's banner (`WorldBannerUI.show`) shows at `TitleAt` (12%), moved under the top bar while the bars are up; (3) **boss** (`BossSeconds` 2.6): a soft fade through black (`CutSeconds` 0.35 each way) to the `BossShot` part (its CFrame is the camera's), **a level shot at the boss's middle height aimed straight at its spawn point** (the user, the same day: a low shot looking up was too low), which pushes in `PushStuds` (3) along its look; **at the start the boss is put back on its spawn point, the middle of its wander circle** (the user: it roamed off-centre of the shot), held there and turned to the shot (`WorldEnemyController.face`, which returns that spot) and plays its attack with its angry face (`EnemyAnimator.showOff`) as the name card slams in from 2.2x; (4) a fade to black (`FadeSeconds` 0.35) and the player is back with the HUD. A click (the gui's `Skip`, after `SkipAfter` 0.5 s) or a death jumps to the fade. **Hand-built `StarterGui/Cinematic`** (IgnoreGuiInset, DisplayOrder 150): `TopBar` / `BottomBar` (black, 0.11 of the screen, slid in from off screen; `BottomBar/SkipHint` "Click to skip"), `Unlocked` (`PopScale`; gold `Rays`, pale `RaysInner`, `World` / `WorldShadow` from `UnlockFormat` "WORLD %d", `Label` / `Shadow` "UNLOCKED!"), `BossCard` (tilted -4, `PopScale`; red `Rays`, `PlateLip`, a purple `Plate`, gold `Name` = the boss's name upper-cased, `SubPill/Sub` from `BossSubtitle` "WORLD BOSS") and `Skip`; gui attributes `UnlockSeconds`, `FlightSeconds`, `BossSeconds`, `BarSeconds`, `FadeSeconds`, `CutSeconds`, `PushStuds`, `BarHeight`, `TitleAt`, `UnlockFormat`, `BossSubtitle`, `SkipAfter`. **Paths:** World 2 (`Straight`, Pitch -26, 7 s) flies dead straight down the world's middle at y 56-76 (P1 (0, 56, -480) to P5 (0, 74, -870)), then cuts to the Elder Treant (`BossShot` (44, 18, -932) looking level at its spawn (68, 18, -932); 24 back so all 20 studs of it fit between the bars after the push); World 3 (`Path`, Pitch -8, 11 s) flies through its gate and mouth, down the way down (y 18 -> -10), through the entry hall, **down the middle of the west lane** (x 179-185; the first version hugged the walls), **diagonally across the middle hall over the lake**, **down the middle of the east lane** (x 329-331) into the boss hall, then cuts to the Spider Queen on her altar ((254, -13, -1449) looking level at (254, -13, -1470)). Checked: rays along both sampled paths hit nothing but the invisible `OverGate` border and World 3's gate sign (gone once bought). A new world needs its own `Cinematic/Flyby` (and nothing in Studio otherwise). **Replay for testing (admin only, my call: the user asked whether players should get it in Settings; a cinematic that only plays when a gate opens stays a moment):** the admin panel's Player page has a `FlybyCard` (a Damage card copy, LayoutOrder 6: a purple `Badge` "W2" from `Admin.Text.flybyBadge`, `Title` "Flyby", `AmountField/Amount` the world, a green `Play`); Play closes the panel and calls `WorldFlybyController.play(world)` 0.3 s later. To fit it, the page's list padding is 0.02 and the Coins / EXP cards 0.18 tall, Level / Health / Damage 0.125. Playtested (HighestWorld lowered and raised on the client): World 3 logged second by second along the lane centres and both boss shots seen; the wandered bosses snapped home ((253.3, -1469.5) -> (254, -1470), (70.6, -933.1) -> (68, -932)) and sat centred in the level shots, World 3's flight ending on the Queen; World 2 at real speed as above, from the World 3 spawn (the far start) too; clean console.
- **No skipping worlds: gate order, invisible borders and the world guard (2026-10-24, the user: players could parkour onto the rim and walk from World 1 into World 2 without buying the gate).** **A gate needs the world before it** (`Config/Gates` `requires`, default `world` - 1, read by `Gates.requires`): with the branching map World 4's gate stands in World 2, so `GateService` refuses ("world") and the sign's Buy reads "World 3 first" (`Gates.Text.needsWorld`) until the player's HighestWorld reaches it. **Invisible borders:** each world has a `Borders` folder of anchored walls (Transparency 1, CanCollide on, CanQuery and CanTouch off, Locked) that stop climbing out: World 1 126 `Column`s, one over every `Ground/Rim` part from its top to y 600; World 2 33 over its hedges, 84 over the cave mouth's `Rock` / `Arch` columns (5 of them lowered onto rock beneath that had left a 2-7 stud gap), and `OverGate` (World 2's gate, from the `Barrier`'s top at 37.6 up, 44.4 wide so it covers the posts and beam); World 3 `OverGate` over its gate (the cave's rock and roof close the rest). A new world needs its own (over its rim and above its gate). **The world guard** (`Server.Services.WorldGuardService`, after PortalService): every 0.25 s it casts a ray down from each player's root into every world's `Ground`; standing on ground of a world above HighestWorld sends them (behind the fade) back to the last spot they stood on in an unlocked world, or to their highest world's `Spawns/PlayerSpawn`. Enemies stay shared server-wide, so sending the player back is the fix (hiding the next world's enemies per player isn't possible). `PortalService.sendTo(player, cframe, opts)` moves to any spot (`send` to a part uses it) and `isBusy(player)` reports a teleport under way. No healing fountains outside World 1 (the user). Playtested: a World 1-only player placed on World 2's path was back on World 1's spot in 0.12 s (HighestWorld restored), walking at a rim column from its bottom stopped 3 studs short and fell, clean console.
- **World 2 stretched to World 1's size (2026-10-20, the user: same length and width as World 1; paths in thirds: one path, the branch, one path into the boss).** Inside the hedges it is now about 264 × 516 (117,872 sq studs; World 1 is 264 × 516, 109,600), from the gate at z −290 to about −808. **Everything older in these World 2 notes gives pre-stretch coordinates.** The method (the user's pick: stretch the layout, not a redesign): x × 1.2692 everywhere; z piecewise so the fork lands at the first third and the rejoin at the second (old → new: −290 → −290, the fork −406 → −462, the rejoin −490 → −634, the arena centre −584 → −728, the south edge −640 → −808). Pieces moved whole (pivot mapped, size and turn kept): trees, ground cover, logs, props, the stalls, enemy markers, critters, fireflies; the camp (cabin, its props, Rowan) moved together by the cabin's mapping (+17, −35), the fairy ring and the lookout by their own (the telescope's `LookAt` / `BasePivot` attributes updated; its view is still clear); the hedge vines were snapped back onto the hedge faces. Boxes mapped: the 32 hedges (`Rim`, edges on 1 stud, plus `Hedge_38` closing the corner south of the cave mouth) and the 411 hill layers (edges on 2 studs; one bottom step by the path removed). Kept rigid: the boss arena, `Ruins` and the Elder Treant marker (+16, −144, centre (68, −728)); the creek, its bridge, water plants and sounds (x mapped, z −124: it keeps its width, at z −604..−589). Rebuilt: `Paths` (the old centre lines mapped and re-drawn with the 12-stud blocky brush on the 4-stud grid, 85 blocks; cells over creek water left open under the bridge; the last leg runs into the arena's west edge), `Grass` (flood from the gate inside the rim, 177 blocks, y 2..8), `PathDetails/Trims` (289, `DevTools.PathTrims`). One spruce moved off the path. Walked: both lanes, the bridge, into the arena and through the cave mouth into World 3; clean console. **World 3 moved with it** (the user's pick): the whole `W03_CrystalCaverns`, `Shared.Gates.World3Gate` and `W02…/Ground/CaveMouth` shifted (+28, 0, −144) as one, with every stored position attribute (`PreRimFixPivot`, `NextAt`, Pip's `PrePosition…`); older World 3 coordinates in these notes are pre-move (the `_W3Grid` dev data in ServerStorage is too). The falling-needle `NeedleSource` was removed (the user). **The hedges are as tall as World 1's cliffs** (the user, the same day): each top mapped from World 2's old 32..48 onto World 1's 36..56 (top' = 36 + (top − 32) × 1.25, rounded), bottoms still at y 2, so tops now run 36..56 averaging 46.2 (World 1 45.6); the 14 hedge vines moved up with their hedges. Backup: `ServerStorage/MapBackup_2026-10-20/W2StretchBefore` (World 2, World 3 and the World 3 gate, untagged). **Decorations topped up to the old density (the same day):** trees 78 → 140 (spruce A 40, B 35, C 34, D 31, scale 0.9–1.1), ground cover 112 → 201 (the same mix of ferns, pinecones, bluebells, clover, mushrooms, foxgloves, glow mushrooms), logs 7 → 12, hedge vines 14 → 21, with the new dev tool `ServerStorage.DevTools.W2Dress` (`begin` / `place(c, templates, folder, n, opts)` / `finish`; opts `kind` tree / cover / log, `footR`, `scale`, `spreadFrom`, `xRange`): best of 30 random candidates farthest from the group's existing pieces, World 2's rules (nothing touching, whole foot on one level of grass or a hill step, trunks ≥ 4 and canopies ≥ 1 from paths, the arena and creek water, out of enemy wander discs + 3, out of the boss sightline boxes and the gate arrival). Vines go on open inward hedge faces, 20+ apart. Checked: trees left 72 / right 68 and 42 / 56 / 42 by thirds, ground cover 95 / 106, logs 6 / 6, vines 11 / 10, 0 trees touching, no log in a wander disc. **Enemy spawns and balance (the same day, the user: World 2's enemies match World 1's, chickens → foxes, bulls → boars, stallions → bears).** Spawns like World 1's: 18 foxes (6 on the first path, 6 down each lane), 6 boars (3 per lane, among the foxes), 4 bears on the last path into the arena, 16+ apart, roaming areas clear (since moved beside the paths: see "Enemies roam beside the paths") (the markers moved and `Fox_11..18`, `Boar_06`, `Bear_03/04` added; 75 world enemies registered). Stats translated from World 1: HP × ~6.9 (the Birch Hatchet's damage per second over the Sickle's) so fights last as long, damage set so an early fight costs ~14% of health; EXP equal to the World 1 counterpart. Fox 276 HP / 20 dmg / 22–45 coins / 10 EXP, Boar 966 / 38 / 90–150 / 38, Bear 1,520 / 50 / 150–226 / 56, Elder Treant 1,200 (kept: the weapon tiers are built on it) / 70 / 338–526 / 150; the Marshmallow Stick drops at 0.1% (secret rule; it was 2%). **Rowan's questline was too short** (players now arrive at level 25, so its level-22 / 27 steps were instant and the line ended by minute 12): steps 5 and 9 are now levels 30 and 34 and the last step is the Elder Treant 5 times (like Dale's), dialogue too. **Measured** (`world_sim.py --world 2`, which now starts a player as they arrive from World 1 with `--from 1`: level 25, ~1,000 coins, the Weathervane Spear, Harvest Knight worn; `world2.json` from `dump_world.luau` with WORLD = 2): **44.3 min typical** from arriving to buying the World 3 gate (fastest quarter 43.6, 9 in 10 by 45.4; 5,000 runs), Rowan done about minute 25, both armours by about 33, then a short bear grind (116 bears on average), 1.1 deaths (0.5 in the first 10 minutes, World 1 0.57), level 45.7 at the gate (36 needed). Playtested: a fox hits for 20, a kill pays 31 coins and 10 EXP. The Index world card was re-shot 2026-10-20.
- **World 2's creek and pond, one water part (2026-10-20, the user: the creek a little thicker, the pond a decent chunk bigger, all one water part like World 1's).** `Ground/Creek/Water` is ONE part (x −130..24, z −606..−582, y 5.5..7.2, 0.2 transparent) and `Creek/Bed` (87 blocks) shapes the water: wherever a cell isn't open water the bed or the bank rises above the water's top, so the slab only shows in the channel (World 1's pond trick; touching see-through parts showed seams). The creek is 7 wide (5 under the bridge, x −53..−33; its bed top 6, colour 56707a), the pond an ellipse at (2, −594), 22 × 12 half-widths (shallow bed top 5.5, 6b8f99; deep top 4, 1e60ac), about 4.6× its old area (1,551 open-water cells in all). Re-cut round it: `Paths` (85), `Grass` (207), `PathDetails/Trims` (291), 7 hill layers, `FireflySource_Pond` (40 × 20 over the pond); the old stepping stones and flow streaks went. Water plants: `Decorations/Water/LilyPads` (8) and `Reeds` (11), 1+ stud from open water; 3 trees that stood in the new water or an enemy spot went (137 left). `W2Dress` reads the water from `Creek/Bed`. Fishing there was added the same day (see "World 2 fishing").
- **The loop hill's bottom step and World 2's logs (2026-10-20, the user's report).** The pond rebuild had removed the loop hill's `Step_1` under its east half (x 0..16), so its level-2 and level-3 slabs floated a stud over the grass (185 cells) with a lone level-1 strip left at x 16..18. Rebuilt as 7 `Step_1` blocks (attribute `Rebuilt`) from z −580 (2 studs off the pond) to 4–8 past the level-2 edge, so the hill steps down one stud at a time again; one reed clump moved along the bank, and a bluebell and a clover left floating on the lost layer sit on it again. **Check after any hill edit:** every `Step_L` cell must have a `Step_(L−1)` under it (0 unsupported now, 0 same-level overlaps). **Every World 2 log floated** (flat ones 0.14–0.17 up: the templates' bark sits above their pivot; the hill-draped ones 0.2–0.5 at their lowest after the stretch moved the steps). - **Path and arena details (2026-10-08, user's request):**
  - `Ground/PathDetails/Trims`: 96 skinny edging strips (0.35 wide, 0.12 proud, 9c7a4e) along every `Ground/Paths` edge that meets grass or a drop. None where a path meets another path, the plaza, a bridge, the arena or a ramp `Step` within 1.1 studs of height.
    - **Rebuilt 2026-10-10 with `ServerStorage.DevTools.PathTrims`** (a dev tool: `loadstring(module.Source)()`, then `.build(opts)`; the old strips are in `ServerStorage.TrimsBackup_2026-10-10`). The user's rules: a strip lies **entirely on its own path block** and **sits on its top** (0.12 tall, nothing below the path top), so it never pokes into the ground or shows in a cliff face. **It never intersects anything.** **Corners (the user's call):** a path's **outer** corners have the normal joined outline; the Z-side strip runs to the corner and the X-side strip stops against it. **Inner** corners, where the path bends round a corner of grass and two blocks' strips meet, keep a small gap where the strips touch only at their corners. Strips are cut around bridges, fences, flowers and stones (all parts are made queryable while scanning), and collinear strips are joined. **Bug fixed 2026-10-10:** the edge test's downward rays used to hit strips laid earlier in the run (and World 1's stepping stones), read them as "not path", and stretched strips half a stud into inner corners. The mace template now really shows its calm face at rest (it still showed angry). - **World 1 balance (2026-10-19, the user's target): about 30 minutes for a typical player** from spawning to buying the World 2 gate, with every weapon except the secret (drops, buys, Dale's spear, the Pumpkin Mace), both armours, Dale's 10 steps and the tools. Game passes, boosts, luck, potions (drinking and brewing), the Corn Cob, the Farm Core and the Corn Wraith are left out (the user's rule). Later worlds get longer, about 45 min, 1 h, 1.5 h, 2 h, 2.5 h, 3 h (memory `world-length-plan`). The numbers: kill coins Chicken 6–12, Bull 24–40, Stallion 40–60, King 90–140; the gate 2,500; Horsehair 50% and Harvest Knight needs 8; stall prices Sickle 150, Machete 400, Pitchfork 650, Shovel 700, Scythe 1,800; the Mace 5% (the user's pick, 2026-10-19, the top of the 1–5% rule) and the Corn Cob 0.1% (rare drop rules: a boss Legendary 1–5%, a secret 0.1%, cores 1% with no luck).
  - **Measured with `art/tools/balance/world_sim.py`** (2026-10-19): it plays a player through the world thousands of times with random drops, walking the real map (marker, node, NPC, stall and gate positions, sprinting, a 1.3 detour), fighting hit by hit (weapon damage, cooldown, hit time, enemy armour, an 85% hit rate; the enemy fights back from the first hit with its damage, windup and cooldown, knockback pushes it out of reach, armour defence and health count), healing at Roblox's default 1% of max HP a second (resting before a fight it can't take, dying costs the 5 s respawn and the walk back, a fight that would take 90% of max HP is skipped for something easier), the per-tier respawns and node respawns, quest steps in order (kills count only after accepting), shop and crafting trips, 15 s per talk with Dale. All game numbers come from `world<N>.json`, which `dump_world.luau` writes from the real configs and the map (run it in Edit mode; it requires fresh copies of the modules because Edit mode caches `require`). `--set` tries changes (`boss.PumpkinMace=0.05`, `gate.coins=2000`, `enemy.Chicken.coins=6-12`, `respawn.boss=30`, `weapon.Scythe.price=1500`, `armor.HarvestKnight.Horsehair=8`), `--trace` prints one run. Multi-target swings don't matter in World 1: markers stand 17+ studs apart (chickens 36+) and a fight pulls only the enemy hit.
  - **Results (2026-10-19):** with the Mace at 5%, a typical player takes **39 min** (fastest quarter 34, slowest quarter 47, 9 in 10 by 58.5; 5,000 runs). The Mace decides it (typical minute 36, about 23 King kills); with it guaranteed the rest takes 30 min (9 in 10 by 36) and coins run out last. Also tried: 3% 46 min, 10% 35, 5% with a 1,500-coin gate 37, 5% with a 15 s King respawn 36. The user chose 5% and kept the rest.
  - **Healing and levels (2026-10-19, the user: new players struggled, healing took too long):** with the healing fountain, level-up heals and the quicker curve (+15 HP a level, bigger armour), a typical run is **32.7 min** (fastest quarter 28.5, slowest quarter 40.6, 9 in 10 by 52; 4,000 runs). Early on a fight takes 14% of max HP on average (a bull 23%, was 36%), deaths fell from about 5.9 a run to 0.4 (0.35 in the first 10 minutes, was 1.3), and resting from 5 min to under 10 s. The simulation heals at the fountain when the detour beats resting, and on every level up. Curves tried (same EXP for every requirement): 100/50 +10 HP 34.6 min with bulls at 36%; 50/25 33.3, 31%; 30/15 +10 32.7, 28%; 30/15 +15 32.6, 23% (chosen); 25/10 32.7, 26%. `--remap` with `--set level.first= level.step= level.hp=` reruns this.
  - **Then (2026-10-19) the World 2 gate at level 25 and 10 HP a level:** typical **35.6 min** (fastest quarter 32.4, slowest quarter 44.8, 9 in 10 by 55.8; 4,000 runs). Level 25 is now the last thing ground out (about 50 Stallion kills, about 2,700 coins left over), early bull fights take 28% of HP, 0.9 deaths a run. With no sprint the same run is 38.8 min; since 2026-10-19 there is no base sprint, so that's the measured time: **39.0 min typical, 9 in 10 by 58** (the Sprint upgrade isn't counted).
  - **The target is now the gate, not the whole world (the user, 2026-10-20):** a typical free player should buy the World 2 gate in **30 minutes or more** (passes and boosts may cut that a lot; the secrets and the Mace keep their rates). `world_sim.py --goal gate` (now the default; `--goal all` is the old measure) ends the run when the gate is bought, but the player still plays normally on the way: Dale's steps, the tools, both armours when it has the level and materials, every drop, and a shop weapon whenever one it can afford hits harder per second than its best; it never chases the Mace or anything else only for completion. Re-read from Studio on 2026-10-20 (the hub stalls had moved): **36.0 min typical**, very tight (fastest quarter 35.2, 9 in 10 by 37.7), because level 25 is the last thing in (about 1,500 coins spare, 52 Stallion kills). **Kill EXP +25% (2026-10-20): Chicken 8 → 10, Bull 30 → 38, Stallion 45 → 56, Scarecrow King 120 → 150** (the Corn Wraith keeps 60): **31.6 min typical** (fastest quarter 30.6, slowest 32.9, 9 in 10 by 34.8; 5,000 runs), 39 Stallion kills, 0.57 deaths, about 1,000 coins spare, all 10 of Dale's steps and 1.6 armour sets done at the gate, levelling about every 75 s. Tried: +10% 34.0, +20% 32.3, +30% 31.1, +35% 30.4. Finishing everything (`--goal all`) is 36.8 min with it. Level 25 stays the gate (the user's question, 2026-10-20): World 2's enemies get tuned to the HP players arrive with (about 340, 415 in Harvest Knight); if World 2 needs more health, raise HP per level or its armour, not the gate level. Playtested: a chicken kill gives 10 EXP.
- **World scale: about x10 a world (2026-10-23, the user: World 1's numbers near 100, World 2 1K, World 3 10K, W4 100K ... W7 100M).** Each world's shape stays as balanced (the notes below give pre-scale numbers); on top of it every world is multiplied by its scale (`Config/Leveling`: `SCALE` {1, 4, 16, 64...} and `scaleAt(level)`, which steps up at each gate's level in `Config/Gates`, bought or not). **x SCALE** (x1 / x4 / x16): enemy damage, armour health, kill and quest EXP, and in code the player's base health and the EXP a level needs (`Leveling.maxHealth` / `need`, so level 24 has 330 HP and needs 375, level 25 1,360 and 1,560, level 50 9,440 and 12,240). **x 2 x SCALE** (x2 / x8 / x32): weapon damage, enemy health, tool power, node health, the Worldheart tiers (`Infuse.DPS` x2, x8, x32, x128...). With the worlds' own growth that is about x10 a world: weapons top out at 94 / 1,024 / 11,424 (the secrets), enemies 80..400 HP in World 1, 904..4,504 in World 2. **Coins are unscaled** (the gates already go 2,500 / 25,000); the coin bundles (`Shop.WorldScale`) and the Upgrades stand (`Upgrades.WORLD_SCALE`) went x10 a world (were 1, 4, 16 / 1, 4, 12...). World 2's hits are x3.46, not x4: Harvest Knight is World 1 armour (unscaled), so the prepared arrival is (340 x 4 + 75) x 1.15 = 1,650 HP, and every World 2 hit keeps its old share of it. **World 3 is provisional** (x32 health, x16 damage and EXP) until it is rebuilt. `world_sim.py` reads the steps from `leveling.scale` (`dump_world.luau` writes them) and has `--probe FILE` (the levels kills, quest hand-ins and worn armour happen at). Measured after: World 1 30.8 min, 0.08 deaths (unchanged); World 2 47.9 min prepared, 1.1 deaths, 53.0 unprepared, 4.4 (unchanged). Playtested: level 26 shows 1,400 / 1,400 HP and 0 / 1,620 EXP, a Dagger hits a chicken (80 HP) for 12 and crits for 18; clean console. **A saved player above level 25 drops levels** (the EXP a level needs grew x4 there); wipe test saves before launch anyway.
- **The rescale from base fights (started 2026-10-24, the user; the rules are the `world-scaling-baseline` memory).** Every world is tuned from one base fight: a player arriving maxed from the world before (World 1: level 1 with the Trowel; World 2: the W2 gate's level, Harvest Knight, upgrades 3, the Pumpkin Mace; World 3: the W3 gate's level, Heartwood Bark, upgrades 6, the Heartwood Staff; no potions or passes) loses about 50% of their health fighting the world's small enemy, and the medium / elite / boss keep today's damage and health ratios to it. A maxed player's DPS (the world's secret weapon + its upgrades, hits only, no Worldheart Blade) is about 100 / 1k / 10k; coins, EXP and prices go x10 a world; a gate every 25 levels; World 1 about 30 min to its gate, later worlds 45-60. `art/tools/balance/base_fights.py` plays the three base fights and prints the ladders and maxed DPS. **Step 1, measured before any change:** base fights 14% / 10% (the Mace's knockback makes the Fox miss) / 24%; maxed DPS 107 / 1,328 / 16,773; World 1 30.8 min, World 2 48.3 min; kill coins, stall prices and upgrades not x10 a world. **Step 2, World 1 (done):** **the level curve is x10 every 25 levels** (`Leveling.GROWTH` = 10^(1/25), +9.6% a level): max HP 100 at level 1, 229 at 10, 912 at 25, 1,000 at 26, 10,000 at 51, each level up adding a little more than the last (the user asked how health keeps up with later enemies; this keeps every world's player health x10 the last with no jump at the gate, where the old 100 + 10 a level x4 / x16 went 330 -> 1,360 at level 25), and a level needs 40 EXP at level 1, x10 every 25 levels (3,366 to level 25, was 4,860). **World 1's enemies hit 3.67x harder:** Chicken 22 (a Trowel fight now costs 50.1% on average, two pecks most fights), Bull 59, Stallion 73, Scarecrow King 81, Corn Wraith 55; health, coins, EXP, prices and level requirements unchanged. `world_sim.py`'s player now rests to 1.5x what a fight should take (`restMargin`, it only rested before a fight that would kill) and skips fights over 70% of max HP (`tooStrong`, was 90%), as a real player does with fights this costly; it reads the new curve (`leveling.growth`). Measured: **29.9 min typical** (fastest quarter 28.8, 9 in 10 by 34.7; 2,000 runs), 1.3 deaths, a chicken fight 21% of max HP on average over the run, bulls first fought around minute 16 (players level on chickens first), stallions late and easy, about 1.3 armour sets and 200 coins spare at the gate. A save's level moves with the curve (the user's 1,426 EXP is level 17, was 10). Playtested: level 17 at 961 HP (437 x the Health upgrade's 2.2), the configs read 22 / 59 / 81; clean console. **`Util.CenterRow` re-entrancy fixed the same day:** two rows of a group could refit each other forever ("Maximum re-entrancy depth (80) exceeded calling task.defer" at join); a refit passed on from the group (`fit(row, true)`) no longer passes its own change back. **Step 3, World 2 (done):** the base arrival is 1,135 HP (912 at level 25 + Harvest Knight's 75, x1.15), taking x0.795, swinging the Mace at 84.5 DPS. **Health x10 World 1's** (Fox 800, Boar 1,900, Bear 2,600, Elder Treant 4,000, Fairy 3,600); **the Fox hits for 80**, 51% over a 10 s fight (tuned without the Mace's knockback: in the game a hit shoves an enemy 10 studs and its attack only lands if it's still in reach, and the simulation's timing version of that swung a fight between 21% and 50% on a 0.1 s windup change, so the knockback is left as the Mace's bonus); the rest keep World 2's ratios (Boar 159, Bear 266, Treant 293, Fairy 199). A Fox hits 3.6x a Chicken, not x10: the arrival's Mace is 2.8x the Trowel against x10 health. **Weapons x0.8** (the Marshmallow Stick at upgrades 6: 1,328 -> 1,062 DPS; Hatchet 141, Cleaver 198, Quarterstaff 211, Mace 237, Woodsman's Axe 563, Greatclub 467, Heartwood Staff 640, Firefly Lantern 762, Marshmallow 819), the Worldheart's World 2 tier 900.9 DPS (819 x 1.1). **Prices, rewards and sells x10 World 1's:** stall weapons 1,500 / 4,000 / 6,500 / 7,000 / 18,000 / 20,000 (sells about a tenth), Woodland Hide 1,500 coins and 350 health, Heartwood Bark 7,500 and 750 (both about a quarter to a third of the level's HP), Stone Axe 500 (power 200, redwoods 1,000 hp: still 5 chops), Stone Rod 750, Rowan's rewards x10 Dale's step for step, World 2 materials and Tier I potions' sells and brews. **Kill EXP x4 and kill coins x3 of World 1's** (Fox 40 / 18-36, Boar 120 / 72-120, Bear 168 / 120-180, Treant 600 / 270-420, Fairy 240 / 75-120): x10 made World 2 a 28-minute world with 20,000 coins spare, because a maxed player kills fast; World 2 is meant to last 45-60. `world_sim.py --upgrades N` now applies **every** upgrade (Sprint everywhere, crits, regen, luck, coins, EXP, gathering; it applied only Health, Defense, Attack and Attack Speed). Measured (`--world 2 --from 1 --upgrades 3 --start-weapons PumpkinMace --start-coins 200`): **48.4 min typical** to the World 3 gate (9 in 10 by 51.9), 0.1 deaths, ~16,000 coins spare toward World 2's upgrades (97,750 for all), the Heartwood Staff in 45% of runs; most of the time is bears (about 200 kills, the best EXP a second); an unprepared arrival (no upgrades, the Spear) 64.9 min, 1.3 deaths. Playtested: the configs and a live Fox read the new numbers; clean console. **Step 4, World 3 (done):** `world3.json` dumped from the new cave for the first time (no gathering or tools yet; its gate is the mock World 4 gate below). The base arrival is 12,831 HP (9,120 at level 50 + Heartwood Bark's 750, x1.3), taking x0.639, swinging the Heartwood Staff at 754 DPS. **Health x10 World 2's** (Cave Bat 8,000, Pebble Golem 19,000, Crystal Golem 26,000, Spider Queen 40,000, Gem Mole 36,000); **the Bat hits for 1,220** (50% over an 11 s fight; the base fights leave knockback out); the rest keep World 3's ratios (2,460 / 4,090 / 4,500, Mole 3,070). **Weapons x0.63** (the Rock Candy Club at upgrades 9: 16,773 -> 10,567 DPS; Batwing Blade 1,230 ... Rock Candy Club 7,197), the Worldheart's tier 3 7,916.7 DPS. **Then x1.15 (2026-10-24, the user's pick):** World 3's rarities had drifted about 20% slower on the Spider Queen than World 2's on the Elder Treant (Common 20.3 s vs 17.7, Secret 6.9 vs 6.1), so every World 3 weapon does x1.15 (Batwing Blade 1,415 ... Rock Candy Club 8,277), the Worldheart's tier 3 9,104.7 DPS; a maxed World 3 player is at 12,152 DPS (the user: only enemy damage has to follow the x10). World 3 is now 50.5 min prepared (was 53.9), 80.0 unprepared (84.8), no deaths prepared. **Prices, rewards and sells x10 World 2's** (stall 15,000 / 40,000 / 65,000 / 70,000 / 180,000 / 200,000; Cave Runner level 53 (58 since the same day, below), 15,000 coins, 3,500 health (5,500); Crystal Queen level 59 (65), 75,000, 7,500 (13,000); Pip's rewards x10 Rowan's; World 3 materials and Tier II potions). **Pip's steps moved for a level-50 arrival:** level steps 55 / 59 (were 36 / 42; 60 / 65 since the same day, below), earn 2,000 (800), give 10,000 (1,500), dialogue too. **The Rock Candy Club drops at 0.1%** (the secrets' rule; it was still 2%). **Kill EXP and coins x5.5 World 2's** (Bat 220 / 100-200, Pebble 660 / 400-660, Crystal 920 / 660-990, Queen 3,300 / 1,500-2,300, Mole 1,320 / 410-660). **The World 4 gate is a mock** (a comment in `Config/Gates`, not a real gate yet): level 75, 250,000 coins, and about 30 + 5 World 3 gathered materials once World 3 has gathering. Measured (`--world 3 --from 2 --upgrades 6 --start-level 50 --start-weapons HeartwoodStaff --start-armor WoodlandHide,HeartwoodBark --start-wear HeartwoodBark --start-coins 1000`): **50.3 min typical** (9 in 10 by 51.4), no deaths, both level and coins binding; unprepared (no upgrades, the Firefly Lantern) 80.5 min. Pip's questline is done by about minute 9; the rest is mostly Crystal Golems (about 234 kills; World 2's end is about 200 bears the same way). `base_fights.py` reads `world3.json` and leaves the base weapon's knockback out. Playtested: the configs and a live Bat read the new numbers; clean console. **The rescale's three base fights now all cost about 50% (50.1 / 51.2 / 50.1%) and maxed DPS is 107 / 1,062 / 10,567.** **The reward ladder (2026-10-24, the user: spread the elite grind; elites stay the best way to level, the boss pays a hefty amount but isn't the best to farm, since its draw is its weapon drops):** per kill, in elites, for EXP and coins alike: **small 0.37, medium 0.7, elite 1, boss 4.3**, the secret enemy 1.4; then each world's whole ladder scaled to its time (World 1 0.75x the old Stallion, Worlds 2 and 3 0.95x the old Bear / Crystal Golem). Per second for a player mid to late in the world (`art/tools/balance/rates_probe.py`, early / mid / late cases): small 64-73%, medium 72-84%, **elite 100%**, boss 77-95% (it has one spot, a long fight and a 30 s respawn, so 3 elites a kill paid only ~60% a second; early in a world the boss pays the most but isn't safe to fight yet). New rewards: Chicken 12 EXP / 9-18 coins, Bull 22 / 18-35, Stallion 32 / 25-50, Scarecrow King 135 / 110-215, Corn Wraith 45 / 35-70; Fox 60 / 35-70, Boar 110 / 65-135, Bear 160 / 95-190, Elder Treant 690 / 410-815, Fairy 225 / 135-265; Cave Bat 320 / 195-385, Pebble Golem 610 / 370-730, Crystal Golem 875 / 525-1,045, Spider Queen 3,760 / 2,260-4,480, Gem Mole 1,225 / 735-1,460. `world_sim.py`'s player now takes the nearest of anything paying within `closeEnough` (10%) of the best (a real player doesn't cross the map for a few percent). Measured: **30.0 / 48.6 / 49.6 min**; a min-maxer still farms the elite, while a player who fights whatever pays within 25% spreads the kills (World 2: 171 boars, 102 bears) and takes 30.7 / 52.9 / 56.1 min. **World 3's spawns re-laid and its nodes put against the walls (2026-10-24, the user: "There are 2 lanes leading to the boss room coming from the middle room. 2 elites should sit at each of these points on the 2 lane", and "the ores need to be moved to the side of the paths").** The two lanes of the second loop each hold two Crystal Golems: the middle (182, -1368) / (326, -1368) and the end by the boss hall (186, -1416) / (322, -1416). The Pebble Golems take those lanes' upper halves ((216, -1300) and (180, -1324) west, (298, -1300) and (332, -1328) east) and the middle of each first-loop lane ((186, -1162) / (326, -1164)); the 18 bats are spread 6 / 6 / 6 through the first loop's lanes (z -1112..-1214) and the middle hall, each moved to the cell farthest from every other marker until none moves. Every roaming circle + 3 is cave floor (at most the 1-stud rough step in it), with nothing solid within it, no gathering node within + 4, clear of the lake and the altar. Nearest neighbours: golems 40-48 studs, pebbles 37-43, bats 23-33 in the first loop's lanes and 35-40 in the middle hall. **Every gathering node now has its back against the cave rock** (the `Ledge` / `Rock` of `Ground/Walls`, 0.4-1 stud off), turned to face the walkway, on the raised step along the walls (y -21); several stood 11-17 studs out on the open floor. Each was moved to the nearest such spot (up to 39 studs away), kept 20+ from the other nodes and out of the way in between the stalls, the boss hall and the two lane ends where the golems stand. Where its footprint crossed the step's 1-stud holes, a `NodePad` under it (`Ground/Floor`, the step's colour 7d7994, top 0.01 under the step) fills them (8 pads). Old node pivots: `ServerStorage/MapBackup_2026-10-24/W3GatheringBeforeWalls`; old spawn pivots `W3SpawnsBefore_2026-10-24` (CFrameValues). (A grouping of the golems in the boss hall came first, with every World 3 kill reward x0.9 to make up for its shorter walks: Cave Bat 290 EXP / 175-345 coins, Pebble Golem 550 / 335-655, Crystal Golem 790 / 470-940, Spider Queen 3,385 / 2,035-4,030, Gem Mole 1,100 / 660-1,315; the user replaced it with this layout and had the original rewards restored the same day.) Measured with the original rewards and no knockback: **48.0 min prepared** (World 2 49.0), 0.24 deaths. Playtested: all 29 regular enemies on their ground, a moved Ore Rock against the island wall from a player's view; clean console.
- **The hard rescale (2026-10-26, the user's playtest feedback: players finished without armour, drops came too easily, enemies were easy to dodge, a maxed World 2 arrival breezed through; "an entire rescaling and make it much harder to progress").** The user's answers: about 2/3 of HP from armour; World 1 45 min, World 2 90 (World 3 ~120); weapon drops at about a quarter; quick enemy windups, more often. **Tools:** `art/tools/balance/rescale.py` reads `before/world<N>.json` (the dumps as they were) and writes new `world<N>.json` from its rules; `tune.sh` holds the tuned knobs; `apply_rescale.py` writes the result into the Luau (Config/Worlds enemies, Weapons, Infuse, Armor, Tools, Gathering, Gates; the questlines are hand-written in Config/Quests and mirrored in rescale's `QUESTS`); `simall.sh` / `simpar.sh` run the simulations. **The rules:** max HP 100 at level 1 to 350 at 25 (`Leveling.HEALTH_KNEE` / `KNEE_HEALTH`), then x10 a world; armour gives about twice a level's health at each gate (Farmhand's Gear 150, Harvest Knight 700...). Base fights: World 1's Chicken takes a level-1 Trowel player to 50%, World 2's Fox and World 3's Cave Bat take a maxed arrival to **75%**. Enemy health ladders small 1, medium 2.4 / 3, elite 3.25 / 4.5, boss 4.0 / 6 (World 1 / Worlds 2-3). Every attack move has a quick windup and cooldown (peck 0.2 / 0.75 ... slam 0.35 / 1.4; `TIMING`), and `EnemyAnimator` plays each attack clip at the speed that lands its strike on the server's windup (`moveSpeed`). Weapon ladder: Common 1, Uncommon 1.12, Rare 1.25, Epic 1.4, **quest Legendary 1.6, boss Legendary 1.75** (the user: the quest one was easier to get and must not be better), Secret 2.0 of the world's Common; the next world's Common is the last boss Legendary x1.15; World 1's Common 31.1 DPS. Weapon drops x0.25, boss Legendaries 2%, secrets 0.1%. **Gathering matters more** (the user: wood and stone came too easily, in big bunches; "not just a killing RPG"): a node gives 1 of its main material a break (was 2-3), and the armour, quests and gates ask for more (World 2 gate 20 Oak + 20 Stone, World 3 gate 35 Redwood + 3 Amber Sap, World 4 gate 30 Crystal Shards + 3 Raw Gems). **Farmhand's Gear is 8 Chicken Feathers + 8 Oak Logs + 75 coins** (the user: hard to get by its level 5; it needed Stone, and the pickaxe only comes at Dale's step 5), so it's crafted about minute 5, before the bulls. **Questlines: no level steps, every line has its elite, counts about half up** (see the questline table). Measured: World 1 46.5 min typical (1.9 deaths), World 2 91.8 (maxed arrival), World 3 117.3. Kill EXP / coins knobs: World 1 x1.2 / x1.6, World 2 x1.12 / x1.12, World 3 x1.5 / x1.1 on the earlier values. Damage numbers are much lower than the 2026-10-24 world scale (weapons about 30-70 DPS in World 1, 60-160 in World 2, 140-370 in World 3). **Playtested (on the user's level-10 save, everything taken back):** a chicken pecks for 15 every 0.76 s, and every attack clip's strike lands on its windup (`moveSpeed` plays them 1.4-2.3x); an oak gave 1 Oak Log; Dale's bring step counted it (3 / 30); the new Reward tab (`QuestTracker/Tracker/Reward`: 0.705 x 0.43 of the tracker at (0.975, 0.885), under the ring badge; `Caption` a CenterRow holding `Label`; `Coins` / `Exp` cream chips, CenterRow with `SizeGroup` QuestRewardTab, a square `Icon` and the `Amount`; `Item` a square chip; old tab in `ServerStorage/MapBackup_2026-10-26/QuestRewardTabBefore`); World 4's warning board hides once World 4 is unlocked; clean console.
- **The user's second round on World 1 (2026-10-26):** **Farmhand's Gear** is 10 Chicken Feathers + 3 Bull Hide + 8 Oak Logs + 75 coins, level 7 (the hide fits its leather look; crafted about minute 20, at the end of Dale's bull step); **Harvest Knight** is 6 Horsehair + the Lucky Horseshoe + 3 Golden Straw + 5 Oak Logs + 15 Stone + 10 Bull Hide + 750 coins, level 16 (its materials, not its level, decide when it's made: about minute 45). **World 1's trees and rocks are 50 HP and the wooden tools hit for 10** (5 hits a node; they were 100 / 120 and 20), every node drop is guaranteed and exactly 1 (only the Gathering upgrade doubles it). **Every enemy material, in every world, is a flat 50% for exactly 1** (cores keep their 1%), and **luck never changes it** (`MaterialService` no longer multiplies by `Bonuses.luck`; the Index shows the plain chance); luck is for weapon drops. **Every material pickup is its own side card** (`MaterialToastUI` no longer adds a repeat onto the card already up). **A tool swung at an enemy** shows the NeedPop where it struck: "Use your weapon!" (NeedPop's `WeaponText`) with the hotbar weapon's picture and "Press 1 for your Machete" / "Tap your..." / "Equip your..." (`ToolHint.weapon`, from `WeaponController`'s tool swing, at most every 1.5 s), and the weapon's hotbar slot shakes (`HotbarUI.nudge("Weapon")`). **The quest card's Reward tab has no "Reward" caption** (it spilled out of the tab; the caption is in `ServerStorage/MapBackup_2026-10-26/QuestRewardCaptionRemoved`): just the Coins / Exp chips (0.36 wide) and the Item chip, centred. Simulated: World 1 about 50 min typical (it was 46.5 before these).
- **World 1's steep ladder and the coin set (2026-10-26, the user's playtest: the first five weapons spanned about 10 DPS and every enemy felt alike; the Bull should be a big step that kills you without the Machete).** `rescale.py`'s `WORLD1_DPS` sets World 1's weapons outright: Dagger 10, Sickle 16, Machete 26, Pitchfork 33, Shovel 42, Scythe 54, Weathervane Spear 66, Pumpkin King's Mace 75, Corn Cob 95 (Rusty Blade 9); the chickens drop the Sickle at 20% and the Machete at 5% again (`WORLD1_DROPS`). **Every World 1 enemy is solved from its own fight** (`WORLD1_FIGHTS`: health set, damage solved for the share of max HP the fight takes): Chicken 35 HP / 12 (the Dagger at level 1: 55%, measured 52%; the Sickle 25-28%), Bull 240 / 17 (the Machete at level 5 with the Patchwork Vest: 82%; the Machete alone 130%, dead; the Sickle with the vest 137%), Stallion 450 / 30 (the Shovel, level 11, Farmhand's Gear: 64%), Scarecrow King 700 / 30 (the Scythe, level 14, Farmhand's Gear: 82%; with Harvest Knight 35%), Corn Wraith 500 / 22. **A third armour set per world, the coin set, first in the Crafter (the user):** World 1's `PatchworkVest` ("Patchwork Vest", Common, level 3, 500 coins and no materials, about 23 chicken kills; +60 health, 5% defence; no model or icon yet, so it only changes the stats). **Farmhand's Gear is buffed** to 220 health and 12% defence (was 150 / 10%). Buying a set no longer puts it on over a better one (`StallService`: it is worn only if its health beats the worn set's). World 2 and 3 follow automatically (World 2's Common is the Mace x1.15 and both are calibrated from their base fights): their enemy health and weapon damage went x1.39, enemy damage unchanged. `world_sim.py`'s dry fight (what decides whether a fight is safe) is now the average of 16 seeded fights (one seeded fight came out lucky and the player took on Bulls that killed nine times in ten), it wears the best set it owns, and World 1's player takes fights up to 92% of its health (the Bull costs 80-90%). Measured: **World 1 55 min typical** (0.27 deaths; held back by the 50 Oak Logs and 50 Stone its quests, armour and gate ask for, not the fights), World 2 108.7, World 3 132.3. Playtested: the Crafter lists Patchwork Vest, Farmhand's Gear, Harvest Knight; bought for 500 coins (worn with no armour, kept off over Farmhand's Gear); the live enemies read 35 / 240 / 450 / 500 HP; the test was taken back; clean console.
- **Second and third rounds the same day (the user's playtest: the Machete alone still beat a Bull at level 7, the Stallion was survivable in the vest).** The Patchwork Vest is level 7, Farmhand's Gear level 12 and 900 coins. `world_sim.py` (`PLAYER.enemyLand`) and `base_fights.py` (`ENEMY_LAND`) count 78% of enemy attacks as landing (the player steps out of some in the game; the user's fights took about that share of what the simulation said), so every enemy hits harder to match. **Three weapons per enemy (the user's design):** each drops a common weapon that makes its own tier quicker (20%), one that just beats the next tier (6%) and a rare one that beats the next tier easily (1%), which the next enemy up drops again as its common one: Chicken Sickle / Machete / Pitchfork, Bull Pitchfork / Shovel / the new Rare, Stallion the new Rare / Scythe / the new Epic, Scarecrow King the new Epic / Pumpkin King's Mace 2% / Corn Cob 0.1% (the Weathervane Spear, Dale's, sits between the new Epic and the Mace). - **Enemy attacks all but always land, and our own shift lock on Ctrl (2026-10-28, the user: players dodged with shift lock; "the attacks are basically guaranteed to hit").** Through its windup an attacking enemy keeps lunging after its target at `Balance.Melee.lungeScale` (2.5) x its chase speed (`MeleeService` `lungeUntil`; it used to stand still, so stepping or strafing away dodged), and the blow reaches `dodgeSlack` 8 studs past its range (was 1.5). Measured: circling a chicken at 30 studs a second for 10 s, 13 of 13 attacks landed. The simulations still count 78% of attacks landing (`PLAYER.enemyLand` / `ENEMY_LAND`), so in the game every fight now costs about a quarter more than they say. **Roblox's shift lock is off** (`StarterPlayer.EnableMouseLockOption` false) and **`Client.Controllers.ShiftLockController`** (after SprintController) is ours: Ctrl (left or right) toggles it; while on, the mouse is locked to the middle (`MouseBehavior.LockCenter`, the locked-cursor icon), the camera sits over the right shoulder (`Humanoid.CameraOffset` 1.75) and the body faces the camera (AutoRotate off, the root turned every frame in a Scheduler Camera system, priority 50); it lets go by itself, remembering it was on, while a panel is open, while talking or in a cinematic (`InputLocked` / `Cinematic`), under a scripted camera and without a living body. Playtested: Ctrl locks it, the body turned with a 70-degree camera turn (facing dot 1.0), F2's admin panel freed the mouse and closing it locked it again.

Measured: the toasts' top 16 px under the card's lowest piece, and back there after the card's gui went off and on.
- **Enemies outrange all but the longest weapons, and run faster each world (2026-10-28, the user).** Each world's config has `attackStart` (World 1 9.5, World 2 10, World 3 10.5 studs past the enemy's front): an enemy starts its attack from there and lunges in to strike (`MeleeService.attackStart`; the lunge closes to its own range), so only the Weathervane Spear (10.5) and Pitchfork (10), the Quarterstaff (11) and Heartwood Staff (10.5), and the Geode Glaive (11.5) can hit from outside it, backing off with about a stud to spare. `chaseScale` (1 / 1.4 / 1.8) multiplies every enemy's chase speed in the world (`MeleeService.chaseSpeed`; the lunge and the walk home too): World 2's Fox 23.8, World 3's Cave Bat 32.4 against the player's walk of 24. Playtested: a chicken started its attack from 9 studs away (a Scythe's reach) and every blow landed.

- **The upgrades are almost required (2026-10-28, the user: scale every world's damage, drops and health around the upgrades).** `rescale.py`'s `fight_upgrades(n, tier)`: every fight assumes the sets of the worlds before complete plus this world's own sets bought as the player climbs (`OWN_LEVELS`: small 0, medium 1, elite 2, secret 2, boss 3 levels of each). Enemy damage is solved with them, so without them a fight costs more than its share (World 1: a Bull 75% -> about 91% without the first Health and Defense levels, the Stallion and the Scarecrow King kill); World 2's and 3's enemy health grows by the damage their Attack / Attack Speed levels add (`up_dps`), so a fight without them lasts longer; World 3's weapon drops are divided by the luck its Luck levels add by then (secrets keep 0.1%), so the rates players see with Luck bought are the table's. **Prices cut to 100 / 250 / 500 a level** x the world's scale (the user: a world's three upgrades about its gate or a little more: 2,550 / 25,500 / 255,000 against gates of 2,500 / 25,000 / 225,000). `world_sim.py` starts each run with the arrival's sets (`START_UPG`), buys this world's levels cheapest first at the stalls (`upgrades_left`, `UPG_PRICES`) and counts them in its coin need: the gate waits until they're all bought. **The drop rule holds in every world** (the user: each enemy drops one weapon that makes it easier, not crazy easier; one that barely beats the next enemy in the right armour; a rare one that beats the next easier): `drop_check` prints all three against their fights; the small enemies' own common drops are now solved too (`WORLD1_EASY` Sickle 40% of a Chicken fight, was 31%; `WORLD_EASY` Hatchet / Batwing Blade 55% against the arrival's 75%, the Quarterstaff / Iron Pickaxe stepped x1.15 from them; World 2's and 3's ladders no longer anchor on the last boss Legendary): every just-beats drop costs 70-75% of the next enemy, every rare one 48-51%. Now, in every world, armour climbs 0 / 10% / 15% / 20% and health climbs with the boss about 1.5x its elite:

Fox 113 HP / 36 dmg, Boar 268 / 72 (12%, not the Bull's 16%: that killed prepared players 2.5 times in the first 10 minutes), Bear 366 / 120, Elder Treant 563 / 132, Fairy 507 / 90. Coins x0.72 (Fox 16-32, Boar 65-108, Bear 108-163, Treant 243-379). `world_sim.py --world 2 --upgrades 3 --start-weapons PumpkinMace`: 43.8 min to the World 3 gate, 0.95 deaths; an unprepared arrival (no upgrades, the Spear) 48.1 min and 5.5 deaths.

- **Bonuses add, and every screen but the Index shows your real numbers (2026-10-23, the user: multiplied, maxed luck reached 16.5x; "all the stats and their multipliers should appear over all UIs").** `Shared.Config.Bonuses` works out each stat's total for the server (the real effect) and every screen (the numbers shown): every source gives a share (x1.5 = +0.5) and a stat is 1 + their sum. Luck / Coins / EXP = 1 + the shop's passes and boosts (ShopService now sums their shares into `LuckBoost` / `CoinBoost` / `ExpBoost`) + the upgrade's share (`Bonuses.luck` / `coins` / `exp`, read by IndexService, MaterialService, CoinService, ExpService and IndexUI's shown chances); damage = 1 + Strength + Attack upgrade, times the admin's `DamageMultiplier` test tool on top (`Bonuses.damage`, MeleeService); speed = 1 + min(Swiftness + armour speed, `Armor.MAX_SPEED`) + the Sprint upgrade's share while sprinting (`Bonuses.speed`, SprintController); max health x the Health upgrade (`Bonuses.health`). **Damage taken still multiplies** (armour, Stoneskin, the Defense upgrade: added, the reductions would reach 98%). Maxed today: luck 4.95x (was 16.5x), coins / EXP 3.71x (7.35x), damage 2.12x (2.41x). `Bonuses.weapon(player, def)` is a weapon as the player swings it (Worldheart tier, Attack Speed upgrade, damage bonuses) and `Bonuses.dps` its damage a second (hits only, no crits: the user's pick). They fill the Inventory's tile pills, sorting, Equip Best, `WeaponStats` card and the Damage / Health / Defense mini cards (Defense is now the whole reduction: armour x Stoneskin x Defense upgrade), the Weaponsmith's chips (filled in `ShopUI.refresh` now, not once), the Crafter's Health card (x the Health upgrade) and the Stats panel (each chip shows its share as an added multiplier, the user's rule, 2026-10-23: "Passes & boosts +1.5×" on a ×2.50 total, "Upgrade +0.15×", "Armor +0.05×"; only Crit Chance, Regen and Gathering stay percentages). **The Index keeps the base numbers** (the user: so players know a weapon's or set's own stats): `WeaponTile.dps(def, true)`. Screens refresh on `Bonuses.Watch` (every attribute a shown stat reads). A new gamepass or boost that raises a stat adds its share in `Bonuses` (or in `Config/Shop` for luck / coins / EXP). **Bug fixed 2026-10-24 (the user: the Index showed almost nothing):** every shared tile worked out a DPS for its pill even when the pill was hidden, and with any Attack Speed upgrade `Upgrades.adjust` multiplied a missing `cooldown` on a material, fish, potion or treasure, so the Index stopped drawing its five item tabs and a boss's drop slip (it never showed in testing because the test saves had Attack Speed at 0). Now `Upgrades.adjust` passes anything without a swing through untouched, and `WeaponTile.fill` works out the DPS only for a pill that shows it. Playtested at Attack Speed 24: every tab full (12 / 27 / 6 / 5 / 16 / 6 / 11 / 3), the Scarecrow King's slip, the Inventory; clean console. Playtested: 2x Luck + 1.5x Luck + VIP = LuckBoost 2.75, with Luck 3 2.90 (the Stats panel's chips +175% / +15%); a Dagger with Attack 3 and Strength I hits 7.44 for 18.6 DPS in the Inventory; the Sickle's Weaponsmith row 15.6 -> 20.2 DPS with Attack Speed 3 too while its Index slip stays 15.6; Health 3 gives 115 max HP; clean console. **The Health icon was re-centred** (its heart sat high: the lobes carry its weight, so it was moved 20 px down onto its shadow, which was redrawn): `rbxassetid://104320402035577` (was `82838416987322`), swapped in `Config/Upgrades` and 9 images in Studio.
- **Stats panel (2026-10-19, option A "stat sheet" on https://claude.ai/artifact/E7RQQXPYH2ZCKBB6V4YaWk; plan in the `stats-leaderboards-plan` memory):** `WeaponHud/SideButtons/StatsButton` (the clipboard, `rbxassetid://90361466146096`, `art/tools/icons/hud_stats_ranks.html` -> `art/ui/hud/hudB_stats.png`) opens `StarterGui/StatsPanel` (`Panel` = "Stats"), wired by `Client.UI.StatsUI`. Every stat is a **total multiplier**, never a percentage (the user's rule), with a chip per source: Max Health (the number: level HP + armour, x the Health upgrade), Luck / Coins / EXP (`LuckBoost` / `CoinBoost` / `ExpBoost` x the upgrade), Damage (Strength x upgrade), Defense (1 / armour's damage x Stoneskin x upgrade), Attack Speed, Regen (the percentage of max HP a second: base 1%, the upgrade's share, a Regen potion's; a percentage since 2026-10-22), Sprint (upgrade x Swiftness; "Locked" before Sprint 1), Gathering, and since 2026-10-22 Crit Damage (the crit multiplier: chips "Base ×1.50", "Upgrade +0.10") and Crit Chance (a percentage: "Base 10% of hits", "Upgrade +3%", total 13%), Gathering (the chance to double: "Chance to double drops", "Upgrade +3%"), the third row (12 rows: the grid's cell is 0.152 tall with 0.0176 gaps, was 0.183 / 0.02 for 10). `Window/Sheet` is a 2-column UIGridLayout of `Templates/StatRow` clones (Tile/Icon, StatName, Parts of `Templates/PartChip`, Total; `UpColor` / `SameColor`); it refreshes every 0.5 s while open. Flat bonuses come first, then multipliers (Max Health is the only stat with one). An admin's own test settings show as an "Admin" chip only while set: "Admin ×10.00" on Damage (`DamageMultiplier`), "Admin override" on Max Health (`MaxHealthOverride`, which replaces the whole max). Playtested: Luck x3.45 from a 3x boost and Luck 3, Defense x1.26 from 18% armour and Defense 3, Regen 2.45% with Regen 3 and a Regen I potion, Gathering 3% (re-checked 2026-10-22; the forced-100% test doubled an oak's 3 logs to 6).
- **Player card and press-E inspect (2026-10-23, the user's picks on https://claude.ai/artifact/MkJ7Xobg1XbSe6zZ8gJxfp: A "profile sheet" for the card, C "peek" for inspecting).** Both read only the shown player's published attributes (Player attributes replicate to every client), so nothing new runs on the server. **`Client.UI.ProfileUI`** fills hand-built `StarterGui/PlayerProfile` (`Panel` = "Profile", a copy of the Stats panel's window; its `Title` is the player's display name): your own card opens from your avatar on the HUD card (`WeaponHud/.../AvatarHolder/ProfileButton`, an invisible button), anyone's from a player list row (`PlayerList/Templates/Row/Hit`) or the inspect card. `Tabs/Tab<Stats|Inventory|Trophies>` (pinned pills, the Ranks panel's period colours as gui `Pill*` attributes, the open one `Selected`). `Identity` (always shown): `Avatar/Photo` (the headshot), `Avatar/LevelBadge` (the HUD badge's copy, kept centred by `PlayerCardUI.watchBadge`), `PlayerName`, `UserName` "@name", `TitleChip` (the worn title or "No title yet"), `Medal` (the player list's chip), `Health` / `XP` (the HUD bars' copies), `CoinsTile` / `IndexTile` (Index progress over every world), `WorldLine`. Stats tab: `Gear/WeaponCard` and `ArmorCard` (red / blue heads; `TileSlot` with the shared tile, `ItemName`, `Rarity` in the gui's `Color_<Rarity>`, `Stat/Value`: the weapon's DPS with THAT player's bonuses (`WeaponTile.dps(def, base, who)`), the set's defence, its health after their Health upgrade), `Gear/ViewInventory` (opens the Inventory tab), and `Stats/Sheet` (a 2-column grid of `Templates/StatLine`: `Icon`, `StatName`, `Total` in `UpColor` / `SameColor`). Inventory tab: `InventoryView/Grid` (the Inventory's square-cell grid) of read-only shared tiles, one per thing for the session (weapons by their DPS, then armour, tools, potions, materials), `Count` (`Format` "%s items"), `Empty`. Trophies tab: `TrophiesView`, the Ranks panel's shelf (kept at its 2.09 shape), filled by `LeaderboardUI.fillTrophies(box, who)` ("Peng's trophies" for someone else). **Layout fixes (2026-10-23, the user's screenshots):** `Tabs` is a copy of the Ranks panel's `Periods` group (the user: "exactly like the Ranks tab"; the same size and spot, its UIListLayout and padding, buttons renamed `Tab<Stats|Inventory|Trophies>`), so a selected pill stays inside the dark bar; with no armour worn, `ItemName` ("No armor") moves to the middle of the card below its head (its authored spot is kept in `BasePosition` / `BaseAnchor`), on both cards; `Health` / `XP` sit at 0.618 / 0.703 of `Identity`, a gap like the HUD card's `BarGap`; each gear card's `ItemName`, `Rarity` and `Stat` are centred in the space right of the tile (x 0.73, 0.46 wide; `Stat` is a `CenterRow` of its icon and number), clear of the tile, and the inspect minis' text is centred beside theirs the same way. Refreshes every 0.5 s while open. **The stat numbers are `Client.Util.StatSheet`** (`measure(who, id)`, `ORDER`, `name`), moved out of StatsUI so the Stats panel and the card always agree. **`Client.UI.InspectUI`:** every other player's character gets a client-side `ProximityPrompt` "Inspect <name>" (E, 10 studs, drawn by PromptController from `Assets/UI/InspectPrompt`, a Talk bubble copy with `TextFormat` "Inspect %s"); it opens hand-built `StarterGui/InspectPeek` (`Panel` = "Inspect": a cream card with `Avatar`, the badge, `PlayerName`, `TitleLine` (gui `TitleFormat` "%s · World %d"), `Health`, `Medal`, `WeaponMini` / `ArmorMini`, `ProfileButton` teal "Full Profile" and `InventoryButton` green, `Close`, `Catcher`, `Blocker`). The two cards share `ProfileUI.fillIdentity` / `fillGear`. **Testing alone in Studio:** set the Studio-only `DevInspectSelf` attribute on your player (Client datamodel) and the prompt goes on your own character. Playtested: the card from the avatar and the player list, all three tabs (57 tiles sorted by DPS), the peek by pressing E, its Inventory button opening the card's Inventory tab; the Stats panel unchanged; clean console. **Two-player test (2026-10-23, Test > Start Test Session > Server & Clients, then Add Clients 2 in the server window; each window is its own MCP Studio instance):** Player1 pressing E on Player2 (level 27, the Mace, Harvest Knight, Attack 6, Luck 3) showed Player2's card: 435 HP, the Mace at 43.7 DPS (37 x 1.18, Player2's bonuses; Player1's own would be 37), Luck x1.15, Damage x1.18, Defense x1.22, 48,250 coins, their 3 items on the Inventory tab; Player2 inspecting Player1 the same way round; a card held open followed the other player's level-up within a second; clean consoles on both clients and the server. Studio's test players have no avatars, so the headshot stays blank there. (The MCP mouse tool's `instance_path` clicks miss buttons near the top of the screen by the 58 px top bar; click at the button's AbsolutePosition centre instead.)

- **Leaderboards (2026-10-19, option A "tabs and podium"):** `WeaponHud/SideButtons/LeaderboardButton` ("Ranks", the podium, `rbxassetid://70437543959926`) opens `StarterGui/LeaderboardPanel` (`Panel` = "Leaderboards"), wired by `Client.UI.LeaderboardUI` (its header lists the names: `Periods`, `Tabs`, `Reset`, `Podium/Place1..3`, `List` of `Templates/Row`, `You`, `Rewards` with `Templates/TierCard` / `Chip`). Boards in `Shared.Config.Leaderboards` (9 until 2026-10-22, 6 since: see Weekly only below): **coins earned** (kills, quests; `CoinService.add`'s `source` "shop" / "admin" / "reward" don't count, nor spending), **levels gained** (`ExpService.onLevelUp`, after `DataLoaded`; all-time ranks by total EXP, shown as the level) and **kills** (every enemy, boss and secret enemy), each Daily (00:00 UTC), Weekly (Monday 00:00 UTC) and All-time; top 50 plus your own row.
  - `Server.Services.LeaderboardService`: counts per player (saved section `leaderboard`: today's / this week's counts with their period, lifetime coins and kills, the reward tag and its expiry, trophies), writes changed scores to OrderedDataStores (`LB_<stat>_<D day|W week|All>`) every 120 s, reads the top 50 every 60 s, answers `Remotes.GetLeaderboard(stat, period)`. When a day or week ends, one server claims it in the `LBRewards` store and posts the top 10's tiers to the `LBMailbox` store; a player's mailbox opens on join (and every minute). Without DataStores (Studio) the boards are this server's players in memory, and the admin remote action `lbReset { period }` pays a period's rewards now.
  - **Weekly only, top 3, and the winners' flair (2026-10-22, the user's picks on https://claude.ai/artifact/Sjkt4yWCvqYH2dWYRot9Vg; this replaces the daily boards and the #4-10 tier below).** `Leaderboards.Periods` is `Weekly` / `All` (the user: daily boards meant "constantly pumping out rewards"); only Weekly pays, to **#1, #2 and #3** (`Leaderboards.Rewards.Weekly`, placeholders: #1 5,000 coins + potions + 30 min 2x Luck and Coins + the chat tag; #2 2,500 + potions + 15 of each material; #3 1,500 + 1,000 EXP + potions + 10 of each), read with `paidPlaces`. A paid place also gives a **medal** (gold #1 / silver #2 / bronze #3, `Leaderboards.Medals`: `place`, `medal` / `cup` images, chip `fill` / `rim`) kept until the next weekly reset and a **trophy** kept for good, per board (`Coins_Gold`). `LeaderboardService.publishFlair` publishes `LeaderMedal` (the best live medal), `LeaderMedalBoard`, `LeaderTrail` (the board of a live #1, the first in `Stats` order) and `Trophies` ("Coins_Gold=2,..."); saved as the section's `medals` / `trophies` (older plain "Gold" counts are dropped). **Medal A over the head:** `Client.Controllers.LeaderFlairController` clones `ReplicatedStorage/Assets/UI/LeaderMedal` (a BillboardGui sized in studs, 1.9, `StudsOffset` 3.1 up, its `Medal` image set per tier) onto every player's Head on every client. **The #1 trail, one per board:** it clones `Assets/Effects/LeaderTrails/<Coins|Levels|Kills>` (an Attachment 0.9 below and 0.7 behind the root holding `Main` (the gold coin / a white star tinted EXP blue / a white spark tinted red to orange) and `Twinkle` emitters, emitting backwards) onto the HumanoidRootPart; its emitters run only while the character moves faster than the trail's `MinSpeed` (2). **Player list chip B:** `PlayerList/Templates/Row/Medal` (a dark pill: `Icon` the medal, `Label` "#1 Levels" in the medal's colour, a matching UIStroke), shown by PlayerListUI while the row's player wears a medal, with `PlayerName` narrowed to its `NarrowWidth` (0.2). **Trophies view B:** the Ranks panel's `Periods/Daily` pill is now `Trophies` (Weekly, All time, Trophies); picking it hides the board (`Tabs`, `Stage`, `Podium`, `ListCard`, `List`, `Empty`, `You`, `Rewards`, `ResetPill`) and shows the hand-built `Window/Trophies` (Title "My trophies", `Note` for no trophies yet, `Row_<board>` with `IconRing/Icon`, `BoardName` and `Gold` / `Silver` / `Bronze` slots of `Cup`, `Count` "x2", `Place`; an empty cup fades to `EmptyCupTransparency` 0.65). The podium's players were already left out of the list (it starts at #4); the Ranks rows have no frames (the user). Art: `art/tools/icons/leader_icons.py` -> `art/ui/leaderboard/medal_*.png` (gold `rbxassetid://75772137275815`, silver `127820547528025`, bronze `73982180655503`), `cup_*.png` (`117647447537398` / `123632312963232` / `123487059752347`) and `art/particles/trail_*.png` (coin `92848809913477`, star `90124408000701`, spark `136054160846342`, twinkle `72200050451759`). (The test tool's raw-coordinate mouse press doesn't reach a GuiButton's InputBegan; press on the button by its instance path.) **Spawn in (issue 25, 2026-10-25, the user's picks: choose a world, and a death respawns you in the world you died in):** `SpawnWorld`, kind `world` (0 = "Newest world", else that world; `Settings.clean` keeps it 0..`MAX_WORLD`, `Settings.worldText`, `Settings.spawnWorld(player, highest)` clamps it to HighestWorld; words in `Settings.Text`). Its hand-built `Row_SpawnWorld` (a Sprint zoom row copy under it; the Graphics section moved down a row and the list's `CanvasRatio` went 1.09 -> 1.182, every row re-placed as a share of the taller canvas; old list in `ServerStorage/MapBackup_2026-10-25/SettingsListBefore`) holds a `Picker` (UIAspectRatioConstraint 7, right edge on the switches') of `Prev` / `Next` (the Worlds panel's gold arrow buttons) round a dark teal `Value` pill; SettingsUI's `wirePicker` steps round Newest world and every world unlocked, wrapping. Icon `art/tools/icons/settings_icons.html` `settings_SpawnWorld` (a map pin on the gold badge) -> `art/ui/settings/settings_SpawnWorld.png`, `rbxassetid://140420527114516`. **`Server.Services.SpawnService`** (after WorldGuardService): every world's `Spawns/PlayerSpawn` is a SpawnLocation since 2026-10-25 (Worlds 2 and 3's were plain parts; same name, size and spot, invisible, no collision; **Duration 0 on all three since 2026-10-25, the user: no spawn protection**, so no force field on spawning (it was 10 s, during which `TakeDamage` did nothing); old parts in `MapBackup_2026-10-25/W0N_PlayerSpawnBefore`), all enabled and neutral, because Roblox ignores a disabled SpawnLocation as a `RespawnLocation` (tested: it spawned in World 1). So every player gets World 1's as `RespawnLocation` the moment they join (never a random pick); once `DataLoaded`, they're sent (no fade, behind the join cover) to the setting's world and that becomes the RespawnLocation; on `Humanoid.Died` it becomes the spawn of the unlocked world they last stood in (`WorldGuardService.worldOf`, kept as they walk; it also exposes `spawnOf` / `worldAt`). Playtested: Newest world with World 3 unlocked joined in the cave; the picker's Next twice read World 1, World 2; a death in World 3 respawned at World 3's spawn, one in World 1 at the hub; after stopping and starting, joined at World 2's spawn; the test settings were put back; clean console.
